If updates are debounce they will be merged.\n   340→\tvar req *model.PushRequest\n   341→\n   342→\tfree := true\n   343→\tfreeCh := make(chan struct{}, 1)\n   344→\n   345→\tpush := func(req *model.PushRequest, debouncedEvents int, startDebounce time.Time) {\n   346→\t\tpushFn(req)\n   347→\t\tupdateSent.Add(int64(debouncedEvents))\n   348→\t\tdebounceTime.Record(time.Since(startDebounce).Seconds())\n   349→\t\tfreeCh <- struct{}{}\n   350→\t}\n   351→\n   352→\tpushWorker := func() {\n   353→\t\teventDelay := time.Since(startDebounce)\n   354→\t\tquietTime := time.Since(lastConfigUpdateTime)\n   355→\t\t// it has been too long or quiet enough\n   356→\t\tif eventDelay >= opts.debounceMax || quietTime >= opts.DebounceAfter {\n   357→\t\t\tif req != nil {\n   358→\t\t\t\tpushCounter++\n   359→\t\t\t\tif req.ConfigsUpdated == nil {\n   360→\t\t\t\t\tlog.Infof(\"Push debounce stable[%d] %d for reason %s: %v since last change, %v since last push, full=%v\",\n   361→\t\t\t\t\t\tpushCounter, debouncedEvents, reasonsUpdated(req),\n   362→\t\t\t\t\t\tquietTime, eventDelay, req.Full)\n   363→\t\t\t\t} else {\n   364→\t\t\t\t\tlog.Infof(\"Push debounce stable[%d] %d for config %s: %v since last change, %v since last push, full=%v\",\n   365→\t\t\t\t\t\tpushCounter, debouncedEvents, configsUpdated(req),\n   366→\t\t\t\t\t\tquietTime, eventDelay, req.Full)\n   367→\t\t\t\t}\n   368→\t\t\t\tfree = false\n   369→\t\t\t\tgo push(req, debouncedEvents, startDebounce)\n   370→\t\t\t\treq = nil\n   371→\t\t\t\tdebouncedEvents = 0\n   372→\t\t\t}\n   373→\t\t} else {\n   374→\t\t\ttimeChan = time.After(opts.DebounceAfter - quietTime)\n   375→\t\t}\n   376→\t}\n   377→\n   378→\tfor {\n   379→\t\tselect {\n   380→\t\tcase <-freeCh:\n   381→\t\t\tfree = true\n   382→\t\t\tpushWorker()\n   383→\t\tcase r := <-ch:\n   384→\t\t\t// If reason is not set, record it as an unknown reason\n   385→\t\t\tif len(r.Reason) == 0 {\n   386→\t\t\t\tr.Reason = model.NewReasonStats(model.UnknownTrigger)\n   387→\t\t\t}\n   388→\t\t\tif !opts.enableEDSDebounce && !r.Full {\n   389→\t\t\t\t// trigger push now, just for EDS\n   390→\t\t\t\tgo func(req *model.PushRequest) {\n   391→\t\t\t\t\tpushFn(req)\n   392→\t\t\t\t\tupdateSent.Inc()\n   393→\t\t\t\t}(r)\n   394→\t\t\t\tcontinue\n   395→\t\t\t}\n   396→\n   397→\t\t\tlastConfigUpdateTime = time.Now()\n   398→\t\t\tif debouncedEvents == 0 {\n   399→\t\t\t\ttimeChan = time.After(opts.DebounceAfter)\n   400→\t\t\t\tstartDebounce = The service registries have the best access to\n   319→\t// locality information, as they can read from various sources (Node on Kubernetes, for example). So while the proxy may not originally have these labels,\n   321→\t// it will by the time we get here (as a result of calling this after SetWorkloadLabels).\n   322→\tproxy.Locality = localityFromProxyLabels(proxy)\n   323→\tif proxy.Locality == nil {\n   324→\t\t// If there is no locality in the registry then use the one sent as part of the discovery request.\n   325→\t\t// This is not preferable as only the connected Pilot is aware of this proxies location, but it\n   326→\t\t// can still help provide some client-side Envoy context when load balancing based on location.\n   327→\t\tproxy.Locality = &core.Locality{\n   328→\t\t\tRegion:  proxy.XdsNode.Locality.GetRegion(),\n   329→\t\t\tZone:    proxy.XdsNode.Locality.GetZone(),\n   330→\t\t\tSubZone: proxy.XdsNode.Locality.GetSubZone(),\n   331→\t\t}\n   332→\t}\n   333→\t// add topology labels to proxy labels\n   334→\tproxy.Labels = labelutil.AugmentLabels(\n   335→\t\tproxy.Labels,\n   336→\t\tproxy.Metadata.ClusterID,\n   337→\t\tutil.LocalityToString(proxy.Locality),\n   338→\t\tproxy.GetNodeName(),\n   339→\t\tproxy.Metadata.Network,\n   340→\t)\n   341→}\n   342→\n   343→func localityFromProxyLabels(proxy *model.Proxy) *core.Locality {\n   344→\tregion, f1 := proxy.Labels[labelutil.LabelTopologyRegion]\n   345→\tzone, f2 := proxy.Labels[labelutil.LabelTopologyZone]\n   346→\tsubzone, f3 := proxy.Labels[label.TopologySubzone.Name]\n   347→\tif !f1 && !f2 && !f3 {\n   348→\t\t// If no labels set, we didn't find the locality from the service registry. We do support a (mostly undocumented/internal)\n   349→\t\t// label to override the locality, so respect that here as well.\n   350→\t\tlocalityLabel := pm.GetLocalityLabel(proxy.Labels)\n   351→\t\tif localityLabel != \"\" {\n   352→\t\t\treturn util.ConvertLocality(localityLabel)\n   353→\t\t}\n   354→\t\treturn nil\n   355→\t}\n   356→\treturn &core.Locality{\n   357→\t\tRegion:  region,\n   358→\t\tZone:    zone,\n   359→\t\tSubZone: subzone,\n   360→\t}\n   361→}\n   362→\n   363→// initializeProxy completes the initialization of a proxy. It is expected to be called only after\n   364→// initProxyMetadata.\n   365→func (s *DiscoveryServer) initializeProxy(con *Connection) error {\n   366→\tproxy := con.proxy\n   367→\t// this should be done before we look for service instances, but after we load metadata\n   368→\t// TODO fix check in kubecontroller treat echo VMs like there isn't a pod\n   369→\tif err := s.WorkloadEntryController.OnConnect(con); err != nil {\n   370→\t\treturn err\n   371→\t}\n   372→\ts.computeProxyState(proxy, nil)\n   373→\t// Discover supported IP Versions of proxy so that appropriate config can be delivered.\n   374→\tproxy.DiscoverIPMode()\n   375→\n   376→\tproxy.WatchedResources = map[string]*model.WatchedResource{}\n   377→\t// Based on node metadata and version, we can associate a different generator.\n   378→\tif proxy.Metadata.Generator != \"\" {\n   379→\t\tproxy.XdsResourceGenerator = s.Generators[proxy.Metadata.Generator]\n   380→\t}\n   381→\n   382→\treturn nil\n   383→}\n   384→\n   385→func (s *DiscoveryServer) computeProxyState(proxy *model.Proxy, request *model.PushRequest) {\n   386→\tproxy.Lock()\n   387→\tdefer proxy.Unlock()\n   388→\tvar shouldResetGateway, shouldResetSidecarScope bool\n   389→\t// 1. If request == nil(initiation phase) or request.ConfigsUpdated == nil(global push), set proxy serviceTargets.\n   390→\t// 2. otherwise only set when svc update, this is for the case that a service may select the proxy\n   391→\tif request == nil || request.Forced ||\n   392→\t\tproxy.ShouldUpdateServiceTargets(request.ConfigsUpdated) {\n   393→\t\tproxy.SetServiceTargets(s.Env.ServiceDiscovery)\n   394→\t\t// proxy.SetGatewaysForProxy depends on the serviceTargets,\n   395→\t\t// so when we reset serviceTargets, should reset gateway as well.\n   396→\t\tshouldResetGateway = true\n   397→\t}\n   398→\n   399→\t// only recompute workload labels when\n   400→\t// 1. stream established and proxy first time initialization\n   401→\t// 2. proxy update\n   402→\trecomputeLabels := request == nil || request.IsProxyUpdate()\n   403→\tif recomputeLabels {\n   404→\t\tproxy.SetWorkloadLabels(s.Env)\n   405→\t\tsetTopologyLabels(proxy)\n   406→\t}\n   407→\t// Precompute the sidecar scope and merged gateways associated with this proxy.\n   408→\t// Saves compute cycles in networking code. Though this might be redundant sometimes, we still\n   409→\t// have to compute this because as part of a config change, a new Sidecar could become\n   410→\t// applicable to this proxy\n   411→\tpush := proxy.LastPushContext\n   412→\tif request == nil {\n   413→\t\tshouldResetSidecarScope = true\n   414→\t} else {\n   415→\t\tpush = request.Push\n   416→\t\tif request.Forced {\n   417→\t\t\tshouldResetSidecarScope = true\n   418→\t\t}\n   419→\t\tfor conf := range request.ConfigsUpdated {\n   420→\t\t\tswitch conf.Kind {\n   421→\t\t\tcase kind.ServiceEntry, kind.DestinationRule, kind.VirtualService, kind.Sidecar:\n   422→\t\t\t\tshouldResetSidecarScope = true\n   423→\t\t\tcase kind.Gateway:\n   424→\t\t\t\tshouldResetGateway = true\n   425→\t\t\tcase kind.Ingress:\n   426→\t\t\t\tshouldResetSidecarScope = true\n   427→\t\t\t\tshouldResetGateway = true\n   428→\t\t\t}\n   429→\t\t\tif shouldResetSidecarScope && shouldResetGateway {\n   430→\t\t\t\tbreak\n   431→\t\t\t}\n   432→\t\t}\n   433→\t}\n   434→\t// compute the sidecarscope for both proxy type whenever it changes.\n   435→\tif shouldResetSidecarScope {\n   436→\t\tproxy.SetSidecarScope(push)\n   437→\t}\n   438→\t// only compute gateways for \"router\" type proxy.\n   439→\tif shouldResetGateway && proxy.Type == model.Router {\n   440→\t\tproxy.SetGatewaysForProxy(push)\n   441→\t}\n   442→\tproxy.LastPushContext = push\n   443→\tif request != nil {\n   444→\t\tproxy.LastPushTime = request.Start\n   445→\t}\n   446→}\n   447→\n   448→// handleWorkloadHealthcheck processes HealthInformation type Url.\n   449→func (s *DiscoveryServer) handleWorkloadHealthcheck(proxy *model.Proxy, req *discovery.DiscoveryRequest) {\n   450→\tif string) {\n   521→\tvar connection *Connection\n   522→\n   523→\tfor _, v := range s.Clients() {\n   524→\t\tif v.proxy.Metadata.ClusterID == clusterID && v.proxy.IPAddresses[0] == ip {\n   525→\t\t\tconnection = v\n   526→\t\t\tbreak\n   527→\t\t}\n   528→\t}\n   529→\n   530→\t// It is possible that the envoy has not connected to this pilot, maybe connected to another pilot\n req.Push.PushVersion)\n   570→\t\tmonServices.Record(float64(totalService))\n   571→\n   572→\t\t// Make sure the ConfigsUpdated map exists\n   573→\t\tif req.ConfigsUpdated == nil {\n   574→\t\t\treq.ConfigsUpdated = make(sets.Set[model.ConfigKey])\n   575→\t\t}\n   576→\t}\n   577→\n   578→\ts.StartPush(req)\n   579→}\n   580→\n   581→// Send a signal to all connections, with a push event.\n   582→func (s *DiscoveryServer) StartPush(req *model.PushRequest) {\n   583→\t// Push config changes, iterating over connected envoys.\n   584→\tif log.DebugEnabled() {\n   585→\t\tcurrentlyPending := s.pushQueue.Pending()\n   586→\t\tif currentlyPending != 0 {\n   587→\t\t\tlog.Debugf(\"Starting new push while %v were still pending\", currentlyPending)\n   588→\t\t}\n   589→\t}\n   590→\treq.Start = time.Now()\n   591→\tfor _, p := range s.AllClients() {\n   592→\t\ts.pushQueue.Enqueue(p, req)\n   593→\t}\n   594→}\n   595→\n   596→func (s *DiscoveryServer) addCon(conID string, con *Connection) {\n   597→\ts.adsClientsMutex.Lock()\n   598→\tdefer s.adsClientsMutex.Unlock()\n   599→\ts.adsClients[conID] = con\n   600→\trecordXDSClients(con.proxy.Metadata.IstioVersion, 1)\n   601→}\n   602→\n   603→func (s *DiscoveryServer) removeCon(conID string) {\n   604→\ts.adsClientsMutex.Lock()\n   605→\tdefer 609→\t\txds.TotalXDSInternalErrors.Increment()\n   610→\t} else {\n   611→\t\tdelete(s.adsClients, conID)\n   612→\t\trecordXDSClients(con.proxy.Metadata.IstioVersion, -1)\n   613→\t}\n   614→}\n env.Register(\"K_REVISION\", \"\",\n\t\"KNative revision, set if running in knative\").Get()\n\n// DiscoveryStream is a server interface for XDS.\ntype DiscoveryStream = xds.DiscoveryStream\n\n// discovery.AggregatedDiscoveryService_DeltaAggregatedResourcesClient\n\ntype Connection struct {\n\txds.Connection\n\n\t// Original node metadata, to avoid unmarshal/marshal.\n\t// This is included in internal events.\n\tnode *core.Node\n\n\t// proxy is the client to which this connection is established.\n\tproxy *model.Proxy\n\n\t// deltaStream is used for Delta XDS. This must be called or future changes may be blocked.\n\tdone func()\n}\n\nfunc newConnection(peerAddr string, stream DiscoveryStream) *Connection {\n\treturn &Connection{\n\t\tConnection: xds.NewConnection(peerAddr, stream),\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc (conn *Connection) Initialize(node *core.Node) error {\n\treturn conn.s.initConnection(node, conn, conn.ids)\n}\n\nfunc (conn *Connection) Close() {\n\tconn.s.closeConnection(conn)\n}\n\nfunc (conn *Connection) Watcher() xds.Watcher {\n\treturn conn.proxy\n}\n\nfunc (conn *Connection) Process(req *discovery.DiscoveryRequest) error {\n\treturn conn.s.processRequest(req, conn)\n}\n\nfunc (conn *Connection) Push(ev any) error {\n\tpushEv := ev.(*Event)\n\terr := conn.s.pushConnection(conn, pushEv)\n\tpushEv.done()\n\treturn err\n}\n\n// processRequest handles one discovery request. This is currently called from the 'main' thread, which also\n// handles 'push' requests and close - the code will eventually call the 'push' code, and it needs more mutex\n// protection. Original code avoided the mutexes by doing both 'push' and 'process requests' in same thread.\nfunc (s *DiscoveryServer) processRequest(req *discovery.DiscoveryRequest, con *Connection) error {\n\tstype := v3.GetShortType(req.TypeUrl)\n\tlog.Debugf(\"ADS:%s: REQ %s resources:%d nonce:%s version:%s \", stype,\n\t\tcon.ID(), len(req.ResourceNames), req.ResponseNonce, req.VersionInfo)\n\tif req.TypeUrl == v3.HealthInfoType {\n\t\ts.handleWorkloadHealthcheck(con.proxy, req)\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\n\t// For now, don't let xDS piggyback debug requests start watchers.\n\tif strings.HasPrefix(req.TypeUrl, v3.DebugType) {\n\t\treturn s.pushXds(con,\n\t\t\t&model.WatchedResource{TypeUrl: req.TypeUrl, ResourceNames: sets.New(req.ResourceNames...)},\n\t\t\t&model.PushRequest{Full: true, Push: con.proxy.LastPushContext, Forced: true})\n\t}\n\n\tshouldRespond, delta := xds.ShouldRespond(con.proxy, con.ID(), req)\n\tif !shouldRespond {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\n\trequest := &model.PushRequest{\n\t\tFull:   true,\n\t\tPush:   con.proxy.LastPushContext,\n\t\tReason: model.NewReasonStats(model.ProxyRequest),\n\n\t\t// The usage of LastPushTime (rather than time.Now()), is critical here for correctness; This time\n\t\t// is used by the XDS cache to determine if a entry is stale. If we use Now() with an old push context,\n\t\t// we may end up overriding active cache entries with stale ones.\n\t\tStart:  con.proxy.LastPushTime,\n\t\tDelta:  delta,\n\t\tForced: true,\n\t}\n\n\t// SidecarScope for the proxy may not have been updated based on this pushContext.\n\t// It can happen when `processRequest` comes after push context has been updated(s.initPushContext),\n\t// but proxy's SidecarScope has been updated(s.computeProxyState -> SetSidecarScope) due to optimizations that skip sidecar scope\n\t// computation.\n\tif con.proxy.SidecarScope != nil && con.proxy.SidecarScope.Version != request.Push.PushVersion {\n\t\ts.computeProxyState(con.proxy, request)\n\t}\n\treturn s.pushXds(con, con.proxy.GetWatchedResource(req.TypeUrl), request)\n}\n\n// StreamAggregatedResources implements the ADS interface.\nfunc (s *DiscoveryServer) StreamAggregatedResources(stream DiscoveryStream) error {\n\treturn s.Stream(stream)\n}\n\nfunc (s *DiscoveryServer) Stream(stream DiscoveryStream) error {\n\tif knativeEnv != \"\" && firstRequest.Load() {\n\t\t// How scaling works in knative is the first request is the \"loading\" request. During\n\t\t// loading request, concurrency=1. Once that request is done, concurrency is enabled.\n\t\t// However, the XDS stream is long lived, so the first request would block all others. As a\n\t\t// result, we should exit the first request immediately; clients will retry.\n\t\tfirstRequest.Store(false)\n\t\treturn status.Error(codes.Unavailable, \"server warmup not complete; try again\")\n\t}\n\t// Check if server is ready to accept clients and process new requests.\n\t// Currently ready means caches have been synced and hence can build\n\t// clusters correctly. Without this check, InitContext() call below would\n\t// initialize with empty config, leading to reconnected Envoys losing\n\t// configuration. This is an additional safety check inaddition to adding\n\t// cachesSynced logic to readiness probe to handle cases where kube-proxy\n\t// ip tables update latencies.\n\t// See https://github.com/istio/istio/issues/25495.\n\tif !s.IsServerReady() {\n\t\treturn status.Error(codes.Unavailable, \"server is not ready to serve discovery information\")\n\t}\n\n\tctx := stream.Context()\n\tpeerAddr := \"0.0.0.0\"\n\tif peerInfo, ok := peer.FromContext(ctx); ok {\n\t\tpeerAddr = peerInfo.Addr.String()\n\t}\n\n\tif err := s.WaitForRequestLimit(stream.Context()); err != nil {\n\t\tlog.Warnf(\"ADS: %q exceeded rate limit: %v\", peerAddr, err)\n\t\treturn status.Errorf(codes.ResourceExhausted, \"request rate limit exceeded: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tids, err := s.authenticate(ctx)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn status.Error(codes.Unauthenticated, err.Error())\n\t}\n\tif ids != nil {\n\t\tlog.Debugf(\"Authenticated XDS: %v with identity %v\", peerAddr, ids)\n\t} else {\n\t\tlog.Debugf(\"Unauthenticated XDS: %s\", peerAddr)\n\t}\n\n\t// InitContext returns immediately if the context was already initialized.\n\ts.globalPushContext().InitContext(s.Env, nil, nil)\n\tcon := newConnection(peerAddr, stream)\n\tcon.ids = ids\n\tcon.s = s\n\treturn xds.Stream(con)\n}\n\n// update the node associated with the connection, after receiving a packet from envoy, also adds the connection\n// to the tracking map.\nfunc (s *DiscoveryServer) initConnection(node *core.Node, con *Connection, identities []string) error {\n\t// Setup the initial proxy metadata\n\tproxy, err := s.initProxyMetadata(node)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\t// Check if proxy cluster has an alias configured, if yes use that as cluster ID for this proxy.\n\tif alias, exists := s.ClusterAliases[proxy.Metadata.ClusterID]; exists {\n\t\tproxy.Metadata.ClusterID = alias\n\t}\n\t// To ensure push context is monotonically increasing, setup LastPushContext before we addCon. This\n\t// way only new push contexts will be registered for this proxy.\n\tproxy.LastPushContext = s.globalPushContext()\n\t// First request so initialize connection id and start tracking it.\n\tcon.SetID(connectionID(proxy.ID))\n\tcon.node = node\n\tcon.proxy = proxy\n\tif proxy.IsZTunnel() && !features.EnableAmbient {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"ztunnel requires PILOT_ENABLE_AMBIENT=true\")\n\t}\n\n\t// Authorize xds clients\n\tif err := s.authorize(con, identities); err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\t// Register the connection. this allows pushes to be triggered for the proxy. Note: the timing of\n\t// this and initializeProxy important. While registering for pushes *after* initialization is complete seems like\n\t// a better choice, it introduces a race condition; If we complete initialization of a new push\n\t// context between initializeProxy and addCon, we would not get any pushes triggered for the new\n\t// push context, leading the proxy to have a stale state until the next full push.\n\ts.addCon(con.ID(), con)\n\t// Register that initialization is complete. This triggers to calls that it is safe to access the\n\t// proxy\n\tdefer con.MarkInitialized()\n\n\t// Complete full initialization of the proxy\n\tif err := s.initializeProxy(con); err != nil {\n\t\ts.closeConnection(con)\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\treturn nil\n}\n\nfunc (s *DiscoveryServer) closeConnection(con *Connection) {\n\tif con.ID() == \"\" {\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\ts.removeCon(con.ID())\n\ts.WorkloadEntryController.OnDisconnect(con)\n}\n\nfunc connectionID(node string) string {\n\tid := atomic.AddInt64(&connectionNumber, 1)\n\treturn node + \"-\" + strconv.FormatInt(id, 10)\n}\n\n// initProxyMetadata initializes just the basic metadata of a proxy. This is decoupled from\n// initProxyState such that we can perform authorization before attempting expensive computations to\n// fully initialize the proxy.\nfunc (s *DiscoveryServer) initProxyMetadata(node *core.Node) (*model.Proxy, error) {\n\tmeta, err := model.ParseMetadata(node.Metadata)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, status.New(codes.InvalidArgument, err.Error()).Err()\n\t}\n\tproxy, err := model.ParseServiceNodeWithMetadata(node.Id, meta)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, status.New(codes.InvalidArgument, err.Error()).Err()\n\t}\n\t// Update the config namespace associated with this proxy\n\tproxy.ConfigNamespace = model.GetProxyConfigNamespace(proxy)\n\tproxy.XdsNode = node\n\treturn proxy, nil\n}\n\n// setTopologyLabels sets locality, cluster, network label\n// must be called after `SetWorkloadLabels` and `SetServiceTargets`.\nfunc setTopologyLabels(proxy *model.Proxy) {\n\t// This is a bit un-intuitive, but pull the locality from Labels first. The service registries have the best access to\n\t// locality information, as they can read from various sources (Node on Kubernetes, for example). They will take this\n\t// information and add it to the labels. So while the proxy may not originally have these labels,\n\t// it will by the time we get here (as a result of calling this after SetWorkloadLabels).\n\tproxy.Locality = localityFromProxyLabels(proxy)\n\tif proxy.Locality == nil {\n\t\t// If there is no locality in the registry then use the one sent as part of the discovery request.\n\t\t// This is not preferable as only the connected Pilot is aware of this proxies location, but it\n\t\t// can still help provide some client-side Envoy context when load balancing based on location.\n\t\tproxy.Locality = &core.Locality{\n\t\t\tRegion:  proxy.XdsNode.Locality.GetRegion(),\n\t\t\tZone:    proxy.XdsNode.Locality.GetZone(),\n\t\t\tSubZone: proxy.XdsNode.Locality.GetSubZone(),\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\t// add topology labels to proxy labels\n\tproxy.Labels = labelutil.AugmentLabels(\n\t\tproxy.Labels,\n\t\tproxy.Metadata.ClusterID,\n\t\tutil.LocalityToString(proxy.Locality),\n\t\tproxy.GetNodeName(),\n\t\tproxy.Metadata.Network,\n\t)\n}\n\nfunc localityFromProxyLabels(proxy *model.Proxy) *core.Locality {\n\tregion, f1 := proxy.Labels[labelutil.LabelTopologyRegion]\n\tzone, f2 := proxy.Labels[labelutil.LabelTopologyZone]\n\tsubzone, f3 := proxy.Labels[label.TopologySubzone.Name]\n\tif !f1 && !f2 && !f3 {\n\t\t// If no labels set, we didn't find the locality from the service registry. We do support a (mostly undocumented/internal)\n\t\t// label to override the locality, so respect that here as well.\n\t\tlocalityLabel := pm.GetLocalityLabel(proxy.Labels)\n\t\tif localityLabel != \"\" {\n\t\t\treturn util.ConvertLocality(localityLabel)\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\treturn &core.Locality{\n\t\tRegion:  region,\n\t\tZone:    zone,\n\t\tSubZone: subzone,\n\t}\n}\n\n// initializeProxy completes the initialization of a proxy. It is expected to be called only after\n// initProxyMetadata.\nfunc (s *DiscoveryServer) initializeProxy(con *Connection) error {\n\tproxy := con.proxy\n\t// this should be done before we look for service instances, but after we load metadata\n\t// TODO fix check in kubecontroller treat echo VMs like there isn't a pod\n\tif err := s.WorkloadEntryController.OnConnect(con); err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\ts.computeProxyState(proxy, nil)\n\t// Discover supported IP Versions of proxy so that appropriate config can be delivered.\n\tproxy.DiscoverIPMode()\n\n\tproxy.WatchedResources = map[string]*model.WatchedResource{}\n\t// Based on node metadata and version, we can associate a different generator.\n\tif proxy.Metadata.Generator != \"\" {\n\t\tproxy.XdsResourceGenerator = s.Generators[proxy.Metadata.Generator]\n\t}\n\n\treturn nil\n}\n\nfunc (s *DiscoveryServer) computeProxyState(proxy *model.Proxy, request *model.PushRequest) {\n\tproxy.Lock()\n\tdefer proxy.Unlock()\n\tvar shouldResetGateway, shouldResetSidecarScope bool\n\t// 1. If request == nil(initiation phase) or request.ConfigsUpdated == nil(global push), set proxy serviceTargets.\n\t// 2. otherwise only set when svc update, this is for the case that a service may select the proxy\n\tif request == nil || request.Forced ||\n\t\tproxy.ShouldUpdateServiceTargets(request.ConfigsUpdated) {\n\t\tproxy.SetServiceTargets(s.Env.ServiceDiscovery)\n\t\t// proxy.SetGatewaysForProxy depends on the serviceTargets,\n\t\t// so when we reset serviceTargets, should reset gateway as well.\n\t\tshouldResetGateway = true\n\t}\n\n\t// only recompute workload labels when\n\t// 1. stream established and proxy first time initialization\n\t// 2. proxy update\n\trecomputeLabels := request == nil || request.IsProxyUpdate()\n\tif recomputeLabels {\n\t\tproxy.SetWorkloadLabels(s.Env)\n\t\tsetTopologyLabels(proxy)\n\t}\n\t// Precompute the sidecar scope and merged gateways associated with this proxy.\n\t// Saves compute cycles in networking code. Though this might be redundant sometimes, we still\n\t// have to compute this because as part of a config change, a new Sidecar could become\n\t// applicable to this proxy\n\tpush := proxy.LastPushContext\n\tif request == nil {\n\t\tshouldResetSidecarScope = true\n\t} else {\n\t\tpush = request.Push\n\t\tif request.Forced {\n\t\t\tshouldResetSidecarScope = true\n\t\t}\n\t\tfor conf := range request.ConfigsUpdated {\n\t\t\tswitch conf.Kind {\n\t\t\tcase kind.ServiceEntry, kind.DestinationRule, kind.VirtualService, kind.Sidecar:\n\t\t\t\tshouldResetSidecarScope = true\n\t\t\tcase kind.Gateway:\n\t\t\t\tshouldResetGateway = true\n\t\t\tcase kind.Ingress:\n\t\t\t\tshouldResetSidecarScope = true\n\t\t\t\tshouldResetGateway = true\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif shouldResetSidecarScope && shouldResetGateway {\n\t\t\t\tbreak\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\t// compute the sidecarscope for both proxy type whenever it changes.\n\tif shouldResetSidecarScope {\n\t\tproxy.SetSidecarScope(push)\n\t}\n\t// only compute gateways for \"router\" type proxy.\n\tif shouldResetGateway && proxy.Type == model.Router {\n\t\tproxy.SetGatewaysForProxy(push)\n\t}\n\tproxy.LastPushContext = push\n\tif request != nil {\n\t\tproxy.LastPushTime = request.Start\n\t}\n}\n\n// handleWorkloadHealthcheck processes HealthInformation type Url.\nfunc (s *DiscoveryServer) handleWorkloadHealthcheck(proxy *model.Proxy, req *discovery.DiscoveryRequest) {\n\tif features.WorkloadEntryHealthChecks {\n\t\tevent := autoregistration.HealthEvent{}\n\t\tevent.Healthy = req.ErrorDetail == nil\n\t\tif !event.Healthy {\n\t\t\tevent.Message = req.ErrorDetail.Message\n\t\t}\n\t\ts.WorkloadEntryController.QueueWorkloadEntryHealth(proxy, event)\n\t}\n}\n\n// DeltaAggregatedResources is not implemented.\n// Instead, Generators may send only updates/add, with Delete indicated by an empty spec.\n// This works if both ends follow this model. On the response it adds 'removed resources' and sends changes for everything.\nfunc (s *DiscoveryServer) DeltaAggregatedResources(stream discovery.AggregatedDiscoveryService_DeltaAggregatedResourcesServer) error {\n\treturn s.StreamDeltas(stream)\n}\n\n// Compute and send the new configuration for a connection.\nfunc (s *DiscoveryServer) pushConnection(con *Connection, pushEv *Event) error {\n\tpushRequest := pushEv.pushRequest\n\n\tif pushRequest.Full {\n\t\t// Update Proxy with current information.\n\t\ts.computeProxyState(con.proxy, pushRequest)\n\t}\n\n\tpushRequest, needsPush := s.ProxyNeedsPush(con.proxy, pushRequest)\n\tif !needsPush {\n\t\tlog.Debugf(\"Skipping push to %v, no updates required\", con.ID())\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\n\t// Send pushes to all generators\n\t// Each Generator is responsible for determining if the push event requires a push\n\twrl := con.watchedResourcesByOrder()\n\tfor _, w := range wrl {\n\t\tif err := s.pushXds(con, w, pushRequest); err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn err\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\tproxiesConvergeDelay.Record(time.Since(pushRequest.Start).Seconds())\n\treturn nil\n}\n\n// PushOrder defines the order that updates will be pushed in. {\n\ts.adsClientsMutex.RLock()\n\tdefer s.adsClientsMutex.RUnlock()\n\treturn len(s.adsClients)\n}\n\nfunc (s *DiscoveryServer) ProxyUpdate(clusterID cluster.ID, ip string) {\n\tvar connection Historically, any LDS change would do a full\n\t// push, recomputing push context. Doing that on every IP change doesn't scale, so we need these to remain\n\t// incremental pushes.\n\t// This allows waypoints only to push LDS on incremental pushes to Address type which would otherwise be skipped.\n\n\t// Waypoints need CDS updates on kind.Address changes\n\t// after implementing use-waypoint which decouples waypoint creation, wl pod creation\n\t// user specifying waypoint use. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-4-6","id":"msg_013xU9n7yerhPnNtwyrvXs7U","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01AhbHYvPRzHHymQyzZuYWms","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/workspace/documentation.md","content":"# Istio Pilot Discovery Service: Architecture Document\n\n## Overview\n\nIstio Pilot's discovery service is the control plane component responsible for translating Istio's high-level configuration (VirtualServices, DestinationRules, ServiceEntries, Gateways, Sidecars) and Kubernetes service registry state into Envoy-native xDS resources, then streaming those resources to all connected proxies over persistent gRPC connections. The subsystem is implemented in `pilot/pkg/xds/` and `pilot/pkg/model/`, with config translation in `pilot/pkg/networking/core/`.\n\nThe four interconnected components are:\n\n1. **DiscoveryServer** — the gRPC server and push orchestrator\n2. **ADS Connection Model** — per-proxy stream lifecycle and push dispatch\n3. **PushContext** — the immutable configuration snapshot and translation layer\n4. **Service Registry** — the service/endpoint discovery layer, including multi-cluster support\n\n---\n\n## 1. DiscoveryServer — The xDS gRPC Server\n\n**Source:** `pilot/pkg/xds/discovery.go`\n\n### Struct Definition\n\n`DiscoveryServer` is the central object. Its key fields reveal the design:\n\n```go\ntype DiscoveryServer struct {\n    Env                 *model.Environment       // access to all Istio config stores\n    Generators          map[string]model.XdsResourceGenerator  // pluggable resource generators\n    ProxyNeedsPush      func(proxy *model.Proxy, req *model.PushRequest) (*model.PushRequest, bool)\n    concurrentPushLimit chan struct{}             // semaphore bounding parallel pushes\n    RequestRateLimit    *rate.Limiter            // per-connection rate limiter\n    pushChannel         chan *model.PushRequest   // unbuffered input to debouncer\n    pushQueue           *PushQueue               // per-proxy ordered push work queue\n    adsClients          map[string]*Connection   // all connected proxies, keyed by connection ID\n    adsClientsMutex     sync.RWMutex\n    Cache               model.XdsCache           // LRU cache for generated xDS resources\n    DebounceOptions     DebounceOptions\n}\n```\n\n### xDS Protocol Support\n\n`DiscoveryServer` registers itself with the gRPC server via `Register`, which calls `discovery.RegisterAggregatedDiscoveryServiceServer`. This means it implements both:\n\n- **State-of-the-World (SotW) ADS**: `StreamAggregatedResources` → `Stream()` in `ads.go`\n- **Incremental (Delta) ADS**: `DeltaAggregatedResources` → `StreamDeltas()` in `delta.go`\n\nBoth modes share the same underlying push path and the same `PushContext`. The difference is in how resource changes are communicated: SotW sends the complete set of resources for a type on every push, while Delta sends only the added/modified and removed resources. Delta mode uses `GenerateDeltas()` on generators (e.g., `EdsGenerator.GenerateDeltas`) which can return both added and removed resources.\n\n### Push Type Classification\n\nThe xDS push type matrix is determined per resource type:\n\n| Resource Type | Triggered by | Full vs. Incremental |\n|---|---|---|\n| CDS (Clusters) | DestinationRule, ServiceEntry, VirtualService (for gateways) | Full push (rebuilds PushContext) |\n| LDS (Listeners) | VirtualService, Sidecar, AuthorizationPolicy, etc. | Full push |\n| RDS (Routes) | VirtualService changes | Full push |\n| EDS (Endpoints) | Pod/Endpoint changes | Incremental push (no PushContext rebuild) |\n\nThe push type is determined by `PushRequest.Full`: `true` means a full push (PushContext is rebuilt, all generators run), `false` means incremental (PushContext reused, only EDS generators run).\n\n### Debouncing\n\nConfig changes arrive via `ConfigUpdate(req *model.PushRequest)`, which writes to `pushChannel`. The `handleUpdates` goroutine runs `debounce()`:\n\n```go\n// pilot/pkg/xds/discovery.go:327\nfunc (s *DiscoveryServer) handleUpdates(stopCh <-chan struct{}) {\n    debounce(s.pushChannel, stopCh, s.DebounceOptions, s.Push, s.CommittedUpdates)\n}\n```\n\nThe debounce function merges incoming `PushRequest` values — if two config changes arrive quickly, their `ConfigsUpdated` sets are unioned and the `Full` flag is OR'd. The debouncer waits `DebounceAfter` (default 100ms) since the last event, or triggers immediately if `debounceMax` (default 10s) has elapsed without relief. EDS pushes bypass debounce entirely (they go directly to `Push()`) unless `enableEDSDebounce` is set.\n\nWhen the debounce timer fires, `Push()` is called:\n\n```go\n// pilot/pkg/xds/discovery.go:258\nfunc (s *DiscoveryServer) Push(req *model.PushRequest) {\n    if !req.Full {\n        req.Push = s.globalPushContext()\n        s.dropCacheForRequest(req)\n        s.AdsPushAll(req)\n        return\n    }\n    // For full push: rebuild PushContext, then broadcast\n    push := s.initPushContext(req, oldPushContext, versionLocal)\n    req.Push = push\n    s.AdsPushAll(req)\n}\n```\n\n### Concurrent Push Limiting\n\nAfter `AdsPushAll` enqueues all connections in the `PushQueue`, the `sendPushes` goroutine dequeues connections and dispatches them:\n\n```go\n// pilot/pkg/xds/discovery.go:457\nfunc doSendPushes(stopCh <-chan struct{}, semaphore chan struct{}, queue *PushQueue) {\n    for {\n        semaphore <- struct{}{}  // blocks when concurrentPushLimit connections are in flight\n        client, push, shuttingdown := queue.Dequeue()\n        go func() {\n            pushEv := &Event{pushRequest: push, done: doneFunc}\n            select {\n            case client.PushCh() <- pushEv:  // delivers to connection's push goroutine\n            case <-closed:\n                doneFunc()\n            }\n        }()\n    }\n}\n```\n\n`concurrentPushLimit` defaults to `features.PushThrottle` (default 100). This prevents the server from overwhelming proxies or its own CPU when the fleet is large.\n\n### Cache Management\n\n`DiscoveryServer.Cache` is a `model.XdsCache` (implemented by `typedXdsCache` in `pilot/pkg/model/typed_xds_cache.go`). Generators (EDS, CDS, RDS) store computed Envoy resources in this cache keyed by a request-specific cache key. On the next push, they first check the cache before recomputing.\n\nCache invalidation is driven by `dropCacheForRequest`:\n\n- **Forced push** (`req.Forced = true`): `ClearAll()` — invalidates everything\n- **Scoped push**: `Clear(req.ConfigsUpdated)` — only entries whose declared dependencies include any of the changed config keys are evicted\n\nEach cached entry declares its dependent configs via `DependentConfigs() []ConfigHash`. The LRU cache tracks this reverse mapping to enable targeted eviction.\n\n### Lifecycle\n\n```\nNewDiscoveryServer()\n    → initJwksResolver()\n\nStart(stopCh)\n    → go WorkloadEntryController.Run()\n    → go handleUpdates()    // debounce goroutine\n    → go periodicRefreshMetrics()\n    → go sendPushes()       // push dispatch goroutine\n    → go Cache.Run()        // cache background work\n\nRegister(grpcServer)\n    → RegisterAggregatedDiscoveryServiceServer()\n\nCachesSynced()             // called when all informers have synced; enables new connections\n\nShutdown()\n    → closeJwksResolver()\n    → pushQueue.ShutDown()\n```\n\nThe server refuses new connections until `CachesSynced()` is called. This ensures proxies do not connect to an empty-cache server and lose their existing configuration (see `ads.go:Stream()` — it returns `codes.Unavailable` if `!s.IsServerReady()`).\n\n---\n\n## 2. ADS and the Connection Model\n\n**Source:** `pilot/pkg/xds/ads.go`, `pilot/pkg/xds/delta.go`, `pilot/pkg/xds/xdsgen.go`\n\n### Stream Establishment\n\nWhen Envoy connects and opens an ADS stream, `StreamAggregatedResources` is invoked, which calls `Stream()`:\n\n```go\n// pilot/pkg/xds/ads.go:188\nfunc (s *DiscoveryServer) Stream(stream DiscoveryStream) error {\n    if !s.IsServerReady() {\n        return status.Error(codes.Unavailable, \"server is not ready\")\n    }\n    // Rate limit new connections\n    if err := s.WaitForRequestLimit(stream.Context()); err != nil {\n        return status.Errorf(codes.ResourceExhausted, ...)\n    }\n    // Authenticate (mTLS SPIFFE identity or JWT)\n    ids, err := s.authenticate(ctx)\n    // Ensure push context exists\n    s.globalPushContext().InitContext(s.Env, nil, nil)\n    // Create connection wrapper\n    con := newConnection(peerAddr, stream)\n    con.ids = ids\n    con.s = s\n    return xds.Stream(con)  // enters the read/push loop in pkg/xds\n}\n```\n\nThe connection is then handed to the generic `xds.Stream()` loop (in `pkg/xds/`), which calls back into Pilot's `Connection` methods for each event.\n\n### The Connection Type\n\n```go\n// pilot/pkg/xds/ads.go:71\ntype Connection struct {\n    xds.Connection                    // base: stream, pushCh, initialized state\n    node        *core.Node            // raw Envoy node proto (for efficient re-serialization)\n    proxy       *model.Proxy          // parsed, enriched proxy metadata\n    deltaStream DeltaDiscoveryStream  // set if using Delta xDS, nil for SotW\n    deltaReqChan chan *discovery.DeltaDiscoveryRequest\n    s           *DiscoveryServer\n    ids         []string              // SPIFFE identities from mTLS certificate\n}\n```\n\n`Connection` wraps the raw gRPC stream with Pilot-specific state. The `proxy` field holds:\n- IP addresses, cluster ID, namespace, service account\n- Proxy type (`SidecarProxy`, `Router` for gateways, `Waypoint`, `Ztunnel`)\n- Computed `SidecarScope` (which services/routes are visible to this proxy)\n- `WatchedResources` map: per-type subscription state (resource names, nonce, version)\n- `LastPushContext` and `LastPushTime` for cache correctness\n\n### Connection Initialization\n\nThe first `DiscoveryRequest` from Envoy carries the `Node` proto with metadata. `initConnection` processes it:\n\n```go\n// pilot/pkg/xds/ads.go:240\nfunc (s *DiscoveryServer) initConnection(node *core.Node, con *Connection, ids []string) error {\n    proxy, err := s.initProxyMetadata(node)  // parse metadata, node ID\n    // resolve cluster aliases\n    proxy.LastPushContext = s.globalPushContext()\n    con.SetID(connectionID(proxy.ID))\n    con.proxy = proxy\n    s.authorize(con, ids)              // check RBAC: is this identity allowed to connect?\n    s.addCon(con.ID(), con)            // register in adsClients map\n    defer con.MarkInitialized()        // unblock any pending pushes for this con\n    s.initializeProxy(con)             // compute SidecarScope, service targets, labels\n    return nil\n}\n```\n\nNote the ordering: `addCon` registers before `initializeProxy` completes. This prevents a race where a new push context is created between registration and initialization — the proxy will receive the push even if it hasn't finished initializing (which is safe because `MarkInitialized` gates the push delivery).\n\n### Request Processing\n\nEach `DiscoveryRequest` from Envoy is dispatched to `processRequest`:\n\n```go\n// pilot/pkg/xds/ads.go:139\nfunc (s *DiscoveryServer) processRequest(req *discovery.DiscoveryRequest, con *Connection) error {\n    // Health check type is handled specially (WorkloadEntry health)\n    if req.TypeUrl == v3.HealthInfoType { ... }\n    // Debug requests get an immediate forced response\n    if strings.HasPrefix(req.TypeUrl, v3.DebugType) { ... }\n\n    // ShouldRespond checks nonce/version to suppress ACK loops\n    shouldRespond, delta := xds.ShouldRespond(con.proxy, con.ID(), req)\n    if !shouldRespond { return nil }\n\n    request := &model.PushRequest{\n        Full:   true,\n        Push:   con.proxy.LastPushContext,\n        Reason: model.NewReasonStats(model.ProxyRequest),\n        Start:  con.proxy.LastPushTime,\n        Delta:  delta,\n        Forced: true,\n    }\n    return s.pushXds(con, con.proxy.GetWatchedResource(req.TypeUrl), request)\n}\n```\n\nThe `ShouldRespond` logic prevents re-sending resources that the proxy already acknowledged. When Envoy sends a request for a type it already has (with a matching nonce), it's treated as an ACK and suppressed.\n\n### Push Dispatch\n\n`pushConnection` is called when the push goroutine receives an `Event`:\n\n```go\n// pilot/pkg/xds/ads.go:472\nfunc (s *DiscoveryServer) pushConnection(con *Connection, pushEv *Event) error {\n    pushRequest := pushEv.pushRequest\n    if pushRequest.Full {\n        s.computeProxyState(con.proxy, pushRequest)  // recompute SidecarScope etc.\n    }\n    pushRequest, needsPush := s.ProxyNeedsPush(con.proxy, pushRequest)\n    if !needsPush { return nil }\n\n    // Push each watched resource type in order: CDS, EDS, LDS, RDS, ...\n    wrl := con.watchedResourcesByOrder()\n    for _, w := range wrl {\n        if err := s.pushXds(con, w, pushRequest); err != nil {\n            return err\n        }\n    }\n    return nil\n}\n```\n\nThe push order — CDS → EDS → LDS → RDS — is important: Envoy processes CDS first (to learn cluster definitions), then EDS (endpoints for those clusters), then LDS (listeners that reference clusters), then RDS (route configs referenced by listeners). Sending in the wrong order would cause Envoy to reference unknown resources.\n\n### `pushXds` — The Generator Dispatch\n\n```go\n// pilot/pkg/xds/xdsgen.go:101\nfunc (s *DiscoveryServer) pushXds(con *Connection, w *model.WatchedResource, req *model.PushRequest) error {\n    gen := s.findGenerator(w.TypeUrl, con)  // lookup: proxy-type/typeURL or just typeURL\n    res, logdata, err := gen.Generate(con.proxy, w, req)\n    if err != nil || res == nil { return err }\n\n    resp := &discovery.DiscoveryResponse{\n        TypeUrl:     w.TypeUrl,\n        VersionInfo: req.Push.PushVersion,\n        Nonce:       nonce(req.Push.PushVersion),\n        Resources:   xds.ResourcesToAny(res),\n    }\n    return xds.Send(con, resp)\n}\n```\n\n### The `StartPush` Broadcast\n\n`StartPush` iterates over all registered connections and enqueues each for a push:\n\n```go\n// pilot/pkg/xds/ads.go:582\nfunc (s *DiscoveryServer) StartPush(req *model.PushRequest) {\n    req.Start = time.Now()\n    for _, p := range s.AllClients() {\n        s.pushQueue.Enqueue(p, req)\n    }\n}\n```\n\n`PushQueue` (`pilot/pkg/xds/pushqueue.go`) is an ordered work queue with merge semantics: if a connection is already pending in the queue, a new enqueue merges the `PushRequest` (OR-ing the `Full` flag, unioning `ConfigsUpdated`). This prevents queue growth when fast-moving config triggers multiple pushes before the first one is dispatched to a slow proxy.\n\n---\n\n## 3. PushContext — Configuration Snapshot and Translation\n\n**Source:** `pilot/pkg/model/push_context.go`, `pilot/pkg/networking/core/`\n\n### What PushContext Holds\n\n`PushContext` is an immutable (after initialization) snapshot of all Istio configuration, pre-indexed for efficient per-proxy lookup:\n\n```go\n// pilot/pkg/model/push_context.go:207\ntype PushContext struct {\n    // Services indexed by exportTo visibility, hostname, namespace\n    ServiceIndex        serviceIndex\n    // VirtualServices indexed by gateway and namespace\n    virtualServiceIndex virtualServiceIndex\n    // DestinationRules indexed by namespace with specificity ordering\n    destinationRuleIndex destinationRuleIndex\n    // Sidecar resources, with lazy-computed defaults per namespace\n    sidecarIndex        sidecarIndex\n    // Gateway resources by namespace\n    gatewayIndex        gatewayIndex\n    // EnvoyFilter patches by namespace\n    envoyFiltersByNamespace map[string][]*EnvoyFilterWrapper\n    // WasmPlugin extensions by namespace\n    wasmPluginsByNamespace  map[string][]*WasmPluginWrapper\n    // Authentication policies (PeerAuthentication, RequestAuthentication)\n    AuthnPolicies       *AuthenticationPolicies\n    // Authorization policies (AuthorizationPolicy)\n    AuthzPolicies       *AuthorizationPolicies\n    // Telemetry resources\n    Telemetry           *Telemetries\n    // Proxy-level config (ProxyConfig)\n    ProxyConfigs        *ProxyConfigs\n    // Mesh-wide config\n    Mesh                *meshconfig.MeshConfig\n    // Metrics/error tracking across this push cycle\n    ProxyStatus         map[string]map[string]ProxyPushStatus\n    // Push version string (used as xDS VersionInfo)\n    PushVersion         string\n}\n```\n\nThe `serviceIndex` pre-computes visibility-based views: `public` (exportTo `*`), `privateByNamespace` (exportTo `.`), `exportedToNamespace` (specific namespace targets). This means per-proxy service list lookups are O(1) hash maps instead of O(N×M) filter loops.\n\nSimilarly, `virtualServiceIndex` maintains `publicByGateway`, `privateByNamespaceAndGateway`, and `exportedToNamespaceByGateway` maps, enabling efficient lookup of which VirtualServices apply to a given gateway or sidecar scope.\n\n`destinationRuleIndex` consolidates multiple DestinationRules that match the same host (via wildcard or specific hostname) into a `ConsolidatedDestRule`, merging their subsets and traffic policies according to the selection priority rules.\n\n### InitContext — Rebuilding the Snapshot\n\n`InitContext` is called by `DiscoveryServer.initPushContext` at the start of each full push. It takes an optional `oldPushContext` to enable incremental rebuilding:\n\n```go\n// pilot/pkg/model/push_context.go:1345\nfunc (ps *PushContext) InitContext(env *Environment, oldPushContext *PushContext, pushReq *PushRequest) {\n    ps.initializeMutex.Lock()   // guard against concurrent init\n    defer ps.initializeMutex.Unlock()\n    if ps.InitDone.Load() { return }  // idempotent\n\n    ps.Mesh = env.Mesh()\n    ps.Networks = env.MeshNetworks()\n    ps.initDefaultExportMaps()\n\n    if pushReq == nil || oldPushContext == nil || !oldPushContext.InitDone.Load() || pushReq.Forced {\n        ps.createNewContext(env)   // full rebuild\n    } else {\n        ps.updateContext(env, oldPushContext, pushReq)  // incremental\n    }\n    ps.InitDone.Store(true)\n}\n```\n\n**Full rebuild** (`createNewContext`) calls:\n1. `initServiceRegistry` — fetches all services from `env.Services()` (the aggregate registry), builds `ServiceIndex`\n2. `initKubernetesGateways` — processes Gateway API resources\n3. `initVirtualServices` — loads all VirtualServices and builds the namespace/gateway indexes\n4. `initDestinationRules` — loads and consolidates all DestinationRules\n5. `initAuthnPolicies`, `initAuthorizationPolicies` — security policy indexes\n6. `initTelemetry`, `initProxyConfigs`, `initWasmPlugins`, `initEnvoyFilters`\n7. `initGateways` — indexes Istio Gateway resources\n8. `initSidecarScopes` — converts `Sidecar` resources into `SidecarScope` objects per namespace\n\n**Incremental rebuild** (`updateContext`) inspects `pushReq.ConfigsUpdated` to determine which sub-indexes need rebuilding:\n\n```go\n// pilot/pkg/model/push_context.go:1397\nfunc (ps *PushContext) updateContext(env, oldPushContext, pushReq) {\n    for conf := range pushReq.ConfigsUpdated {\n        switch conf.Kind {\n        case kind.ServiceEntry, kind.DNSName:\n            servicesChanged = true\n        case kind.DestinationRule:\n            destinationRulesChanged = true\n        case kind.VirtualService:\n            virtualServicesChanged = true\n        case kind.Gateway:\n            gatewayChanged = true\n        case kind.Sidecar:\n            sidecarsChanged = true\n        // ... etc\n        }\n    }\n    // Only rebuild changed sub-indexes; copy the rest from oldPushContext\n    if servicesChanged { ps.initServiceRegistry(...) } else { ps.ServiceIndex = oldPushContext.ServiceIndex }\n    // ... similarly for each sub-index\n    // Sidecar scopes are rebuilt if services, virtual services, destination rules, OR sidecars changed\n    if servicesChanged || virtualServicesChanged || destinationRulesChanged || sidecarsChanged {\n        ps.initSidecarScopes(env)\n    }\n}\n```\n\nThis optimization avoids scanning all VirtualServices if only a DestinationRule changed, for example.\n\n### Config Translation: VirtualService → RouteConfiguration\n\nThe translation from Istio resources to Envoy resources happens in `pilot/pkg/networking/core/`. The key interface is:\n\n```go\n// pilot/pkg/networking/core/configgen.go:28\ntype ConfigGenerator interface {\n    BuildListeners(node *model.Proxy, push *model.PushContext) []*listener.Listener\n    BuildClusters(node *model.Proxy, req *model.PushRequest) ([]*discovery.Resource, model.XdsLogDetails)\n    BuildDeltaClusters(proxy *model.Proxy, updates *model.PushRequest, watched *model.WatchedResource) (...)\n    BuildHTTPRoutes(node *model.Proxy, req *model.PushRequest, routeNames []string) ([]*discovery.Resource, model.XdsLogDetails)\n    BuildNameTable(node *model.Proxy, push *model.PushContext) *dnsProto.NameTable\n    BuildExtensionConfiguration(node *model.Proxy, push *model.PushContext, ...) []*core.TypedExtensionConfig\n}\n```\n\n`ConfigGeneratorImpl` (in `pilot/pkg/networking/core/configgen.go`) implements this interface. Generators in `pilot/pkg/xds/` call into it:\n\n- `LdsGenerator.Generate` → `configgen.BuildListeners`\n- `CdsGenerator.Generate` / `GenerateDeltas` → `configgen.BuildClusters` / `BuildDeltaClusters`\n- `RdsGenerator.Generate` → `configgen.BuildHTTPRoutes`\n- `EdsGenerator.Generate` / `GenerateDeltas` → endpoint builder in `pilot/pkg/xds/endpoints/`\n\n**VirtualService → RouteConfiguration:**\n`BuildHTTPRoutes` iterates over VirtualServices applicable to the proxy (from `proxy.SidecarScope` for sidecars, or `PushContext.VirtualServicesForGateway` for gateways). For each VirtualService, it produces an Envoy `RouteConfiguration` containing `VirtualHost` entries. HTTP match conditions (`headers`, `uri`, `method`), retries, timeouts, fault injection, and traffic mirroring defined in the VirtualService spec are translated directly into Envoy's `route.Route` proto. Destination host+subset references become Envoy cluster names in the form `outbound|<port>|<subset>|<hostname>`.\n\n**DestinationRule → Cluster:**\n`BuildClusters` calls the cluster builder (`pilot/pkg/networking/core/cluster_builder.go`) for each service visible to the proxy. The cluster builder:\n1. Creates an Envoy `Cluster` for each service port (named `outbound|<port>||<hostname>`)\n2. Looks up the applicable `DestinationRule` via `PushContext.destinationRule(proxyNamespace, service)`, which applies the specificity and namespace priority rules\n3. Translates `TrafficPolicy` (connection pool settings, circuit breakers, load balancing, TLS mode) into Envoy cluster fields\n4. For each DR subset, creates an additional cluster named `outbound|<port>|<subset>|<hostname>` with the subset's overrides applied\n\n**EnvoyFilter Patching:**\nAfter generating base resources, `envoyfilter` patches (`MERGE_PATCH`, `ADD_TO_ARRAY`, `REPLACE`, `REMOVE`) from `EnvoyFilter` resources are applied to modify or extend the generated Envoy proto messages. This is the primary extension point for custom Envoy configuration.\n\n### DestinationRule Selection Priority\n\nThe `destinationRule()` function (`pilot/pkg/model/push_context.go:1244`) implements a four-level priority:\n1. Namespace-local rules in the proxy's own namespace\n2. Rules in the service's namespace (exported to the proxy's namespace)\n3. Rules in the root (config) namespace\n\nThis allows platform operators to define mesh-wide defaults in `istio-system` while allowing teams to override for their own services.\n\n### Metrics and Error Tracking\n\n`PushContext` implements `Metrics` to collect per-push status:\n\n```go\nfunc (ps *PushContext) AddMetric(metric monitoring.Metric, key string, proxyID, msg string)\n```\n\nGenerators call `AddMetric` to record conditions like:\n- `EndpointNoPod` — an endpoint IP with no matching pod (usually transient during pod startup)\n- `ProxyStatusNoService` — a proxy not selected by any Service\n- `DuplicatedSubsets` — conflicting subset definitions across DestinationRules\n- `ProxyStatusClusterNoInstances` — a cluster with zero healthy endpoints\n\nAt the end of each push cycle, `UpdateMetrics` flushes these counters to Prometheus. The debug endpoint (`/debug/push-status`) exposes the last push's `ProxyStatus` map as JSON.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Service Registry and Multi-Cluster Support\n\n**Source:** `pilot/pkg/serviceregistry/`, `pilot/pkg/serviceregistry/kube/controller/controller.go`, `pilot/pkg/serviceregistry/aggregate/controller.go`, `pilot/pkg/xds/eds.go`\n\n### The ServiceRegistry Interface\n\nThe core abstraction is `serviceregistry.Instance`:\n\n```go\n// pilot/pkg/serviceregistry/instance.go:25\ntype Instance interface {\n    model.Controller      // Run(stop), AppendServiceHandler, AppendWorkloadHandler, HasSynced\n    model.ServiceDiscovery // Services(), GetService(), InstancesByPort(), GetProxyWorkloadLabels()\n    Provider() provider.ID\n    Cluster() cluster.ID\n}\n```\n\n`model.ServiceDiscovery` is the query interface that `PushContext.initServiceRegistry` uses to enumerate services. `model.Controller` provides the event handler registration points used to trigger xDS pushes.\n\n### The Kubernetes Controller\n\n`pilot/pkg/serviceregistry/kube/controller/Controller` is a `serviceregistry.Instance` backed by Kubernetes informers:\n\n```go\n// pilot/pkg/serviceregistry/kube/controller/controller.go:189\ntype Controller struct {\n    client     kubelib.Client\n    queue      queue.Instance          // work queue for event processing\n    namespaces kclient.Client[*v1.Namespace]\n    services   kclient.Client[*v1.Service]\n    endpoints  *endpointSliceController\n    nodes      kclient.Client[*v1.Node]\n    pods       *PodCache\n    servicesMap map[host.Name]*model.Service  // hostname -> Service\n    // ...\n}\n```\n\nThe controller watches:\n- **Services**: converted to `model.Service` objects with port mappings, labels, and visibility attributes\n- **EndpointSlices** (via `endpointSliceController`): reconciled against Pod state to produce `model.IstioEndpoint` objects carrying IP, port, labels, locality, and health status\n- **Pods** (via `PodCache`): used to enrich endpoints with workload labels (for traffic policy matching) and locality information from the node's `topology.kubernetes.io/region` and `topology.kubernetes.io/zone` labels\n- **Nodes**: used to build node-IP maps for NodePort service translation and multi-network gateway detection\n\nWhen a Service or EndpointSlice event is received, the controller calls back into `XDSUpdater` (the `DiscoveryServer`):\n\n```go\n// endpoint change → EDSUpdate\ns.XDSUpdater.EDSUpdate(shard, hostname, namespace, endpoints)\n\n// service change → SvcUpdate + ConfigUpdate\ns.XDSUpdater.SvcUpdate(shard, hostname, namespace, event)\ns.XDSUpdater.ConfigUpdate(&PushRequest{Full: true, Reason: ServiceUpdate})\n```\n\n### The Aggregate Controller\n\n`pilot/pkg/serviceregistry/aggregate/Controller` fans out queries to all registered registries and merges their results:\n\n```go\n// pilot/pkg/serviceregistry/aggregate/controller.go:42\ntype Controller struct {\n    registries        []*registryEntry  // ordered: Kubernetes first, then ServiceEntry\n    configClusterID   cluster.ID\n    handlers          model.ControllerHandlers\n    handlersByCluster map[cluster.ID]*model.ControllerHandlers\n}\n```\n\n`AddRegistryAndRun` adds a new registry (e.g., a newly discovered remote cluster) and starts it. The aggregate controller propagates handler registrations to each registry so that service and network gateway changes in any sub-registry trigger the appropriate `XDSUpdater` callbacks.\n\n`ServiceDiscovery` methods (`Services()`, `GetService()`, `InstancesByPort()`) are implemented by iterating over all registries and merging results, with deduplication where needed (e.g., `AddressInformation` for ambient mode cross-checks registry results).\n\n### Multi-Cluster Support\n\nMulti-cluster is implemented via the `multicluster.Controller` (in `pkg/kube/multicluster/`), which watches the secret-based kubeconfig registry. When a new cluster secret is created, `multicluster.Controller` creates a new `kubecontroller.Controller` for that cluster and calls `aggregate.Controller.AddRegistryAndRun`. Each remote cluster's controller uses its own Kubernetes client but shares the same `XDSUpdater` (the `DiscoveryServer`).\n\nThis means:\n- Services from all clusters are visible in `PushContext.ServiceIndex`\n- Each remote cluster's endpoints are tracked in a separate `ShardKey` within `EndpointIndex`\n- Cluster aliasing (`ClusterAliases` in `DiscoveryServer`) allows a proxy reporting cluster ID `A` to be treated as belonging to cluster ID `B`, enabling cluster renaming scenarios\n\nThe `configClusterID` field in `aggregate.Controller` identifies the primary cluster — the one from which Istio CRDs (VirtualService, DestinationRule, etc.) are read. For `ServiceEntry`-defined services, the ServiceEntry controller runs only on the config cluster.\n\n### Endpoint Updates and EDS Push Path\n\nWhen a Pod is added, deleted, or changes readiness state, the Kubernetes controller reconciles the EndpointSlice and calls `XDSUpdater.EDSUpdate`:\n\n```go\n// pilot/pkg/xds/eds.go:47\nfunc (s *DiscoveryServer) EDSUpdate(shard model.ShardKey, serviceName string, namespace string,\n    istioEndpoints []*model.IstioEndpoint,\n) {\n    pushType := s.Env.EndpointIndex.UpdateServiceEndpoints(shard, serviceName, namespace, istioEndpoints, true)\n    if pushType == model.IncrementalPush || pushType == model.FullPush {\n        s.ConfigUpdate(&model.PushRequest{\n            Full:           pushType == model.FullPush,\n            ConfigsUpdated: sets.New(model.ConfigKey{Kind: kind.ServiceEntry, Name: serviceName, Namespace: namespace}),\n            Reason:         model.NewReasonStats(model.EndpointUpdate),\n        })\n    }\n}\n```\n\n`EndpointIndex.UpdateServiceEndpoints` stores the endpoint shard and returns whether the update requires an incremental or full push:\n- **Incremental EDS push** (`Full: false`): endpoint IPs/ports changed but the service structure did not. Only EDS resources are regenerated; the PushContext is not rebuilt.\n- **Full push** (`Full: true`): returned when a new shard is created (i.e., first endpoints for a service), which can affect cluster discovery (CDS).\n\nThis distinction is central to scalability: most pod churn (rolling deployments, health changes) generates incremental EDS pushes that bypass debouncing and PushContext rebuilding entirely.\n\n`EDSCacheUpdate` is a variant that updates the endpoint shard without triggering a push — used by batch reconciliation paths that will trigger a single push at the end.\n\n### CDS vs. EDS Trigger Distinction\n\n| Change | Push Type | Reason |\n|---|---|---|\n| Pod added/removed (existing service) | Incremental EDS | Endpoint shard updated; cluster structure unchanged |\n| New service created | Full push (CDS+EDS+LDS) | New cluster must be created in Envoy |\n| Service deleted | Full push | Existing cluster must be removed |\n| DestinationRule changed | Full push (CDS) | Cluster circuit breaker / LB config changes |\n| VirtualService changed | Full push (LDS+RDS) | Listener/route config changes; EDS unaffected |\n| EnvoyFilter changed | Full push (all) | May patch any resource type |\n\n---\n\n## Data Flow: Config Change to xDS Push\n\nThe end-to-end flow from an Istio config change to Envoy receiving updated xDS:\n\n```\n1. User applies a VirtualService CRD change\n        ↓\n2. Kubernetes informer in config controller fires\n        ↓\n3. DiscoveryServer.ConfigUpdate() called with PushRequest{Full:true, ConfigsUpdated:{VirtualService/foo}}\n        ↓\n4. pushChannel receives the PushRequest\n        ↓\n5. debounce() accumulates events (DebounceAfter=100ms)\n        ↓\n6. Push(req) called:\n   a. initPushContext() → NewPushContext() → InitContext()\n      - updateContext() detects virtualServicesChanged=true\n      - Rebuilds only virtualServiceIndex; copies other indexes from old PushContext\n   b. dropCacheForRequest() → Cache.Clear({VirtualService/foo}) — evicts cached RDS entries\n   c. AdsPushAll(req) → StartPush(req) — enqueues all connections in PushQueue\n        ↓\n7. sendPushes() dequeues connections (up to concurrentPushLimit in parallel):\n   For each Connection:\n   a. pushConnection() → computeProxyState() — recomputes SidecarScope if needed\n   b. ProxyNeedsPush() — checks if this proxy is affected by ConfigsUpdated\n      (proxies in unrelated namespaces with Sidecar scoping may be skipped)\n   c. For each WatchedResource in PushOrder:\n      - LdsGenerator.Generate() → configgen.BuildListeners(proxy, push)\n        → fetches applicable VirtualServices from virtualServiceIndex\n        → builds Listener with HCM, route config references\n      - RdsGenerator.Generate() → configgen.BuildHTTPRoutes(proxy, push, routeNames)\n        → fetches VirtualServices, translates to Envoy RouteConfigurations\n        → checks Cache (miss due to eviction in step 6b)\n        → generates new RouteConfiguration protos\n        → stores in Cache\n      - CDS and EDS skipped (ldsNeedsPush/cdsNeedsPush returns false for pure VS change)\n   d. DiscoveryResponse sent over gRPC stream\n        ↓\n8. Envoy receives DiscoveryResponse, applies new listeners and routes\n9. Envoy ACKs with DiscoveryRequest (same nonce) → ShouldRespond returns false → ignored\n```\n\n---\n\n## Extension Points\n\n### Custom Resource Generators\n\nThe `Generators` map in `DiscoveryServer` maps type URLs to `model.XdsResourceGenerator` implementations. This is how new xDS resource types are added:\n\n```go\ntype XdsResourceGenerator interface {\n    Generate(proxy *model.Proxy, w *model.WatchedResource, req *model.PushRequest) (Resources, XdsLogDetails, error)\n}\n```\n\nPilot registers built-in generators for CDS, LDS, RDS, EDS, NDS (NameTable), SDS, ECDS (ExtensionConfig), and WorkloadEntry/WorkloadAuthorization types. Custom generators can be registered to handle proprietary type URLs. The `findGenerator` lookup order is:\n1. `proxy.Metadata.Generator + \"/\" + typeURL` (explicit per-connection override)\n2. `proxy.Type + \"/\" + typeURL` (per-proxy-type override, e.g. only for gateways)\n3. `typeURL` (global)\n4. `proxy.XdsResourceGenerator` (connection default, if set in node metadata)\n\nThis allows, for example, a custom generator for MCP (Mesh Configuration Protocol) type URLs to be registered for specific proxy types.\n\n### EnvoyFilter Patching\n\n`EnvoyFilter` resources are the primary extension mechanism for modifying generated Envoy configuration without forking Pilot. They apply `MERGE_PATCH`, `ADD_TO_ARRAY`, `REPLACE`, or `REMOVE` operations to any generated proto at any stage of the listener/cluster/route build. Patches are matched by context (SIDECAR_INBOUND, SIDECAR_OUTBOUND, GATEWAY) and by listener name, cluster name, or route configuration name.\n\nThe patch logic is in `pilot/pkg/networking/core/envoyfilter/`.\n\n### WasmPlugin Extensions\n\n`WasmPlugin` resources allow injecting WebAssembly-based Envoy HTTP or Network filters. These are resolved during `initWasmPlugins` and applied during `BuildListeners` as additional filter entries. The `ECDS` (Extension Config Discovery Service) xDS resource type is used for dynamic Wasm plugin distribution, allowing plugin binaries to be updated without restarting proxies.\n\n### External Service Registries\n\n`serviceregistry.Instance` is an interface. Any service discovery system can implement it (the codebase includes `memory`, `mock`, and `serviceentry` implementations alongside the primary `kube` one). External registries integrate by:\n1. Implementing `model.ServiceDiscovery` for service/endpoint queries\n2. Implementing `model.Controller` for event handler registration\n3. Calling `XDSUpdater.EDSUpdate` / `ConfigUpdate` when their state changes\n4. Registering with `aggregate.Controller.AddRegistryAndRun`\n\n### Telemetry API\n\n`Telemetry` resources are processed by `initTelemetry` and applied during listener construction. They configure per-proxy access logging, tracing, and metrics provider settings without requiring EnvoyFilter patches. The telemetry system is pluggable via the provider model — `wellknownProviders` in `push_context.go` lists all supported telemetry backends (Zipkin, Datadog, OpenTelemetry, Prometheus, etc.).\n\n---\n\n## Error Handling\n\n### Config Validation Errors\n\nConfig validation happens at admission time via the Istio validation webhook — by the time a resource reaches `PushContext.InitContext`, it has already passed structural validation. However, semantic errors (referencing a non-existent service, conflicting timeout values) are caught during `initVirtualServices` / `initDestinationRules` and logged as warnings. Invalid configs are typically skipped rather than crashing the push, to minimize blast radius.\n\n### Push Generation Failures\n\n`pushXds` returns any error from `gen.Generate()`. If a generator returns an error (e.g., proto marshal failure), `pushConnection` propagates it, causing the stream to be torn down. Envoy will reconnect and get fresh configuration on the next stream. Generator panics are not currently recovered — they would crash the goroutine and produce a stream closure. Individual push failures do not block other connections' pushes (each runs in its own goroutine under the semaphore).\n\n### gRPC Send Failures\n\nIf `xds.Send(con, resp)` fails (client disconnected, stream broken), the error propagates from `pushXds` → `pushConnection` → the send goroutine, which returns it. The `xds.Stream` read loop (in `pkg/xds/`) detects the error, calls `Connection.Close()` which invokes `closeConnection`, removing the connection from `adsClients`. The `removeCon` function logs an error if the connection ID was not found (which would indicate a bookkeeping bug). After removal, the next `StartPush` will not enqueue this connection.\n\n### Proxy Disconnect Handling\n\n`WorkloadEntryController.OnDisconnect(con)` is called in `closeConnection`. This handles the case of auto-registered `WorkloadEntry` proxies (VM workloads): if a VM's proxy disconnects, its WorkloadEntry health status is updated. For Kubernetes sidecar proxies, disconnect simply removes the connection from `adsClients`.\n\n### Server Not Ready\n\nBefore `CachesSynced()` is called, `Stream()` and `StreamDeltas()` return `codes.Unavailable`. This prevents reconnected Envoys from receiving empty configuration (all services unknown) during Istiod startup. Kubernetes readiness probes check `IsServerReady()` so that the load balancer does not route Envoy connections to Istiod instances still warming up.\n\n### Cache Eviction on Push Failure\n\nIf a push is started but the proxy disconnects mid-push, the `done` callback (`doneFunc` in `doSendPushes`) is called anyway (from the `case <-closed` branch). This calls `queue.MarkDone(client)`, which checks if a new push was queued during the in-flight push (merging it) and re-enqueues if so. This prevents the proxy from being permanently stuck in the `processing` state in `PushQueue`.\n\n---\n\n## Relevant Source Files\n\n| File | Purpose |\n|---|---|\n| `pilot/pkg/xds/discovery.go` |
